\displaystyle\phi_{\theta}(s)=\int_{\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\theta}(a^{\prime}|s)\phi(s,a^{\prime})\mathrm{d}a^{\prime},\quad\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}=Q^{\theta}\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)-r_{h}^{(k)}-\gamma\int_{\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\theta}\left(a^{\prime}\left|s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right.\right)Q^{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)},a^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}a^{\prime},

and

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\theta}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\left(\varepsilon^{\theta}_{h,1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)\right)^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}\left(\varepsilon^{\theta}_{h,1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)\right].

We first give the finite sample guarantee.

Theorem D.3

(Finite Sample Guarantee)
.

For any
t\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, when
K\geq 36\kappa_{1}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})^{2}C_{1}d(1-\gamma)^{-2}\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}
and
\lambda\leq\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta}C_{1}d\sigma_{\min}(\Sigma)
, with probability
1-\delta
, we have,

\displaystyle|\langle t,\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\rangle|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t}{HK}\cdot\log\frac{8}{\delta}}+\frac{C_{\theta}\|t\|\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}}{HK},

where
C_{\theta}=240C_{1}dm^{0.5}(1-\gamma)^{-3}\kappa_{1}(5+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\left(\max_{j\in[m]}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|+\frac{G}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\right)
and

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{\max}\left(\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma_{\theta}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)}{\sigma_{\min}\left(\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma_{\theta}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\wedge 1},\quad\kappa_{2}=\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(1)}\right)\phi_{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\right]\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\kappa_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{G}\max_{j\in[m]}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\phi_{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(1)}\right)\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\phi_{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(1)}\right)\right)^{\top}\right]\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Theorem D.4

(Finite Sample Guarantee - Reward Free)
.

Let the conditions in Theorem
D.3
hold, with probability
1-\delta
, we have for any reward function
r
,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}^{j}v_{\theta}\right|\leq 4b_{\theta}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{8m}{\delta}}{HK}}+\frac{2C_{\theta}\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}}{HK},\quad\forall j\in[m],

where
b_{\theta}=\frac{G}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}\right\|
and
C_{\theta}
is the same as that in Theorem
D.3
. If we in addition have
\phi(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s,a)\geq 0,\forall(s,a),(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}^{j}v_{\theta}\right|\leq\frac{16G}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{8m}{\delta}}{HK}}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|\log(C_{1}d)+\frac{2C_{\theta}\sqrt{m}\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}}{HK},\quad\forall j\in[m].

The complete proofs of Theorem
D.3
and Theorem
D.4
are deferred to Appendix
D.6.1
and
D.6.2
. Next we show that FPG is an asymptotically normal and efficient estimator.

Theorem D.5

(Asymptotic Normality)
.

The FPG estimator is asymptotically normal:

\displaystyle\sqrt{HK}\left(\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(0,\Lambda_{\theta}).

The proof of Theorem
D.5
is deferred to Appendix
D.6.3
. An obvious corollary of Theorem
D.5
is that for any vector
t\in\mathbb{R}^{m},

\sqrt{HK}\left\langle t,\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\right\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t\right).

The following theorem states the Cramer Rao bound for FPG estimation.

Theorem D.6
.

Let Assumption
D.1
hold. For any vector
t\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, the variance of any unbiased estimator for
t^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}
is lower bounded by
\frac{1}{\sqrt{HK}}t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t.

The proof of Theorem
D.6
is deferred to Appendix
D.6.4
.

D.3
Additional Notations

Define

\displaystyle\widehat{r}(\cdot,\cdot)

\displaystyle:=\phi(\cdot,\cdot)^{\top}\widehat{w}_{r},

\displaystyle U^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\gamma\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)Q^{\theta},

\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)Q^{\theta},

\displaystyle\tilde{U}^{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)\widehat{Q}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\Delta Y_{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{HK}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\phi_{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}-\Sigma M_{\theta}

\displaystyle\Delta\Sigma^{-1}

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}-\Sigma.

When
\mathcal{F}
is the class of the linear functions, there exists matrix
M_{\theta}
such that the transition probability satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\phi(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})^{\top}|s,a\right]=\phi(s,a)^{\top}M_{\theta}.

D.4
Technical Lemmas

Lemma D.7
.

We have

\displaystyle Q^{\theta}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}r,\quad\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}U^{\theta}.

Proof.

By Bellman’s equation, we have
Q^{\theta}=r+\gamma\mathcal{P}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}
, which implies

\displaystyle Q^{\theta}=r+\gamma\mathcal{P}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}=r+\gamma\mathcal{P}_{\theta}r+\gamma^{2}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{2}Q^{\theta}=\ldots=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}r,

which proves the first equation. Differentiating on both sides of the Bellman’s equation w.r.t.
\theta
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}(s,a)=\gamma\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\pi_{\theta}(a^{\prime}|s^{\prime})\right)Q^{\theta}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})|s,a\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})|s,a\right],

i.e.,
\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}=U^{\theta}+\gamma\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}
. By induction, we have proved the second equation.
∎

The decomposition leads to the following boundedness result:

Lemma D.8
.

We have
|Q^{\theta}(s,a)|\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma},\ \left\|\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}(s,a)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{G}{(1-\gamma)^{2}},\ \forall s\in\mathcal{S},a\in\mathcal{A}.

Lemma D.9
.

For any series of matrices
A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{n}
and
\Delta A_{1},\Delta A_{2},\ldots\Delta A_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}+\Delta A_{i})-\prod_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\right\|\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\|A_{i}\|+\|\Delta A_{i}\|\right)-\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|A_{i}\|.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{i=1}^{n}(A_{i}+\Delta A_{i})-\prod_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{n}\setminus\{(1,1,\ldots,1)\}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}^{\delta_{i}}(\Delta A_{i})^{1-\delta_{i}}\right\|\leq\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{n}\setminus\{(1,1,\ldots,1)\}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|A_{i}\|^{\delta_{i}}\|\Delta A_{i}\|^{1-\delta_{i}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\|A_{i}\|+\|\Delta A_{i}\|\right)-\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|A_{i}\|.

∎

The following lemma gives an upper bound on the 2-norm of
M_{\theta}
and its derivatives.

Lemma D.10
.

We have
\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 1
and
\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq G,\ \forall j\in[m]
.

Proof.

Note that for any
f:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},\ f(s,a):=\mu^{\top}\phi(s,a)
, and any fixed
h\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[f^{2}(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[f^{2}(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{h},a_{h}\right]|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[f(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{h},a_{h}]^{2}|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}].

The LHS satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[f^{2}(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}]

\displaystyle=\mu^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}\mu,

and the RHS satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[f(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{h},a_{h}]^{2}|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}]=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[\mu^{\top}M_{\theta}^{\top}\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\phi(s_{h},a_{h})^{\top}M_{\theta}\mu|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}]=\mu^{\top}M_{\theta}^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}M_{\theta}\mu.

Therefore, we have
\mu^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}\mu\geq\mu^{\top}M_{\theta}^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}M_{\theta}\mu,\ \forall\mu
, which implies
\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\leq 1
. Similarly, let
g:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},\ g(s,a):=\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\log\pi_{\theta}(s,a)\right)\mu^{\top}\phi(s,a)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[g^{2}(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[g^{2}(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{h},a_{h}]|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[g(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{h},a_{h}]^{2}|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}].

The LHS satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[g^{2}(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}]

\displaystyle=\mu^{\top}\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\log\pi_{\theta}(a|s)\right)^{2}\phi(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})\phi(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})^{\top}|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}\right]\mu

\displaystyle\leq G^{2}\mu^{\top}\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\phi(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})\phi(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})^{\top}|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}\right]\mu=G^{2}\mu^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}\mu,

and the RHS satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[g(s_{h+1},a_{h+1})|s_{h},a_{h}]^{2}|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}[\mu^{\top}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)^{\top}\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\phi(s_{h},a_{h})^{\top}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)\mu|s_{1}\sim\xi_{\theta}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{\top}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)\mu.

Therefore, we get
G^{2}\mu^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}\mu\geq\mu^{\top}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)\mu,\ \forall\mu
, which implies
\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq G
.
∎

D.5
Probabilistic Events

We define the following probabilistic events:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{HK}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}-I_{d}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{8dH}{\delta}}{K}}+\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{8dH}{\delta}}{3K}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Y,0}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq\left(\kappa_{2}\vee 1\right)\sqrt{\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{16dH}{\delta}}{K}}+\frac{4C_{1}d\log\frac{16dH}{\delta}}{3K}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Y,j}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq\left(\kappa_{3}\vee 1\right)G\sqrt{\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{16mdH}{\delta}}{K}}+\frac{4C_{1}dG\log\frac{16mdH}{\delta}}{3K}\right\},j\in[m],

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Y}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{j=0}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{Y,j}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,0}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{KH}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{1-\gamma}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2\log\frac{32dH}{\delta}}{KH}}+\frac{2\sqrt{C_{1}d}\log\frac{32dH}{\delta}}{K\sqrt{H}}+\frac{2C_{1}d\left(\log\frac{32dH}{\delta}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{3K^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{H}}\right)\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,j}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{KH}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right\|\leq\frac{2\sqrt{d}G}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}}{KH}}+\frac{2\sqrt{C_{1}d}\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}}{K\sqrt{H}}+\frac{2C_{1}d\left(\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{3K^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{H}}\right)\right\},j\in[m],

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle:=\bigcap_{j=0}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,j},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{E}_{\Sigma}\cap\mathcal{E}_{Y}\cap\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}.

We have the following guarantees on the above high probability events:

Lemma D.11
.

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\Sigma}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{4}
.

Proof.

Define

\displaystyle X^{(k)}=\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}.

It’s easy to see that
X^{(1)},X^{(2)},\ldots,X^{(K)}
are independent and
\mathbb{E}\left[X^{(k)}\right]=I_{d}
. In the remaining part of the proof, we will apply the matrix Bernstein’s inequality to analyze the concentration of
\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}X^{(k)}
. We first consider the matrix-valued variance
\textrm{Var}\left(X^{(k)}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(X^{(k)}-I_{d}\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(X^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right]-I_{d}
. Let

\displaystyle\Phi^{(k)}:=\left[\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{1},a^{(k)}_{1}\right),\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{2},a^{(k)}_{2}\right),\ldots,\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{H},a^{(k)}_{H}\right)\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times H},

Then
X^{(k)}=\frac{1}{H}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{(k)}\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. For any vector
\mu\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mu^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(X^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right]\mu=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X^{(k)}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{H^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{(k)}\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{H^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{(k)}\right\|^{2}\left\|\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{C_{1}d}{H}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{1}d\mu^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[X^{(k)}\right]\mu=C_{1}d\|\mu\|^{2},

where we used the identity
\left\|\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}=\mu^{\top}X^{(k)}\mu
and
\mathbb{E}\left[X^{(k)}\right]=I_{d}
. We have

\displaystyle\textrm{Var}(X^{(k)})\preceq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(X^{(k)}\right)^{2}\right]\preceq C_{1}dI_{d}.

Additionally,

\displaystyle-I_{d}\preceq X^{(k)}-I_{d}=\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}-I_{d}\preceq C_{1}dI_{d}-I_{d}.

Therefore,
\|X^{(k)}-I_{d}\|\leq C_{1}d
. Since
X^{(1)},X^{(2)},\ldots,X^{(K)}
are
i.i.d.
, by the matrix-form Bernstein inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}X^{(k)}-I_{d}\right\|\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq 2d\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}/2}{C_{1}dK+C_{1}d\varepsilon/3}\right),\quad\forall\varepsilon>0,

i.e., with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{4}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(X^{(k)}-I_{d}\right)\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{8d}{\delta}}{K}}+\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{8d}{\delta}}{3K},

which has finished the proof.
∎

Lemma D.12
.

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{Y}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{4}
.

Proof.

Take

\displaystyle Y_{\theta}^{(k)}:=\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)\phi_{\theta}\left(s^{(k)}_{h+1}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad\forall k\in[K].

Then,
\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
. Note that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]=&\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}\left[\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)\phi_{\theta}\left(s^{(k)}_{h+1}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\\
=&\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}\left[\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\phi\left(s^{\prime},a^{\prime}\right)^{\top}|s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right]\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\\
=&\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}\left[\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h}\right)^{\top}M_{\theta}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]=\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{aligned}

(24)

To this end,
\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right)
. Since the trajectories are i.i.d., we use the matrix-form Bernstein inequality to estimate
\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Delta Y_{\theta})\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|
. Let

\displaystyle\Phi^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\left[\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{1},a^{(k)}_{1}\right),\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{2},a^{(k)}_{2}\right),\ldots,\phi\left(s^{(k)}_{H},a^{(k)}_{H}\right)\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times H},

\displaystyle\Phi^{(k)}_{\theta}

\displaystyle:=\left[\phi_{\theta}\left(s^{(k)}_{2}\right),\phi_{\theta}\left(s^{(k)}_{3}\right),\ldots,\phi_{\theta}\left(s^{(k)}_{H+1}\right)\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times H},

We have
Y^{(k)}_{\theta}=\frac{1}{H}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{(k)}\left(\Phi^{(k)}_{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. For any
\mu\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right]\mu=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{H^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi_{\theta}^{(k)}\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{H^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi_{\theta}^{(k)}\right\|^{2}\left\|\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}d}{H}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}d}{H}\mu^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi^{(k)}\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right]\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{1}d\|\mu\|^{2},

where we have used the fact
\Sigma=\frac{1}{H}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi^{(k)}\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right]
. It follows that

\displaystyle\textrm{Var}_{1}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right):=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right)\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right)^{\top}\right]\preceq\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right]\preceq C_{1}dI_{d}.

Analogously,

\displaystyle\textrm{Var}_{2}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right):=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right)^{\top}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right)\right]\preceq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]

\displaystyle\preceq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}d}{H}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\Phi^{(k)}_{\theta}\left(\Phi^{(k)}_{\theta}\right)^{\top}\right]\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore,
\max\left\{\left\|\textrm{Var}_{1}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\right\|,\left\|\textrm{Var}_{2}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\right\|\right\}\leq C_{1}d\left(\kappa_{2}^{2}\vee 1\right)
. It also holds that
\|Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\|\leq C_{1}d
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 2C_{1}d.

Applying Matrix Bernstein’s inequality, we derive for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\|>\varepsilon\right)\leq 2d\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}/2}{C_{1}dK\left(\kappa_{2}^{2}\vee 1\right)+2C_{1}d\varepsilon/3}\right),

which implies
\mathcal{E}_{Y,0}
holds with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{8}
. For
\mathcal{E}_{Y,j},j\in[m]
, notice that for any
j\in[m]
, we have
\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}(\Delta Y_{\theta}))\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right)
, and
\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}=\frac{1}{H}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{(k)}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\Phi_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. For any
\mu\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right]\mu=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{H^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\Phi_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{H^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\Phi_{\theta}^{(k)}\right\|^{2}\left\|\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right].

Since we have

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\phi_{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right)\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\phi_{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\theta}\left(a\left|s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right.\right)\pi_{\theta}\left(a^{\prime}\left|s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right.\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\log\pi_{\theta}\left(a\left|s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right.\right)\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\log\pi_{\theta}\left(a^{\prime}\left|s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right.\right)\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\phi\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)},a\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)},a^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}a^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle G^{2}\int_{\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\theta}\left(a|s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right)\pi_{\theta}\left(a^{\prime}|s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right)\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)},a\right)\right\|\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)},a^{\prime}\right)\right\|\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}a^{\prime}\leq G^{2}C_{1}d,

which implies

\displaystyle\mu^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right]\mu\leq\frac{G^{2}C_{1}d}{H}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\Phi^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mu\right\|^{2}\right]=G^{2}C_{1}d\|\mu\|^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\textrm{Var}_{1}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right):=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right)^{\top}\right]\preceq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right]\preceq G^{2}C_{1}dI_{d}.

Meanwhile, we have

\displaystyle\textrm{Var}_{2}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\preceq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\preceq\frac{C_{1}d}{H}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\Phi_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\Phi_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right]\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

In conclusion, we get

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\|\textrm{Var}_{1}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\right\|,\left\|\textrm{Var}_{2}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right)\right\|\right\}\leq G^{2}C_{1}d\left(\kappa_{3}^{2}\vee 1\right),

Note that
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right\|\leq C_{1}dG
, we know
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{\theta}^{(k)}\right]\right\|\leq 2C_{1}dG
. By Matrix Bernstein’s inequality, we get for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}Y_{k}^{\theta}-\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right\|\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq 2d\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}/2}{G^{2}C_{1}dK\left(\kappa_{3}^{2}\vee 1\right)+2C_{1}dG\varepsilon/3}\right),

taking a union bound over all
j\in[m]
proves that
\bigcap_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{Y,j}
holds with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{8}
. Using a union bound argument again, we know with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{4}
,
\mathcal{E}_{Y}
holds, which has finished the proof.
∎

Lemma D.13
.

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon})\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{4}
.

Proof.

Let
X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}:=\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let
\mathcal{F}_{h,k}
be
\sigma
-algebra generated by the history up to step
h
at episode
k
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]=0
. We apply matrix-form Freedman’s inequality to analyze the concentration property. Consider conditional variances
\textrm{Var}_{1}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]:=\mathbb{E}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\left(X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]:=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\in\mathbb{R}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\left\|\textrm{Var}_{1}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|=

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\left(X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\left(X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right\|\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]=\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]

and

\displaystyle\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]=\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\textrm{Var}\left[\left.\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right|s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right),

where we have used
\textrm{Var}\left[\left.\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right|s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right]\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
. Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)=

\displaystyle KHd+KH\textrm{Tr}\left(\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{HK}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}-I_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle KHd+KHd\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{1}{HK}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}-I_{d}\right\|.

We take

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}:=\frac{KHd}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(1+\sqrt{\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{32dH}{\delta}}{K}}+\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{32dH}{\delta}}{3K}\right).

(25)

The result of Lemma
D.11
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{1}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\leq\sigma^{2}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{16}.

(26)

Additionally, we have
\left\|X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{C_{1}d}}{1-\gamma}
. The Freedman’s inequality therefore implies that for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\geq\varepsilon,\ \max\left\{\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{1}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\}\leq\sigma^{2}\right)\\
\leq&2d\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}/2}{\sigma^{2}+\sqrt{C_{1}d}\varepsilon/(3(1-\gamma))}\right),\end{aligned}

(27)

where
\sigma^{2}
is defined in (
25
). We take

\displaystyle\varepsilon:=\sigma\sqrt{2\log\frac{32d}{\delta}}+\frac{2\sqrt{C_{1}d}}{3(1-\gamma)}\log\frac{32d}{\delta}.

Then we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\geq\varepsilon,\ \max\left\{\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{1}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\}\leq\sigma^{2}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{16},

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\geq\varepsilon,\ \max\left\{\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{1}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\}\leq\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\max\left\{\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{1}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|,\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\}>\sigma^{2}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{8}.

which has proved
\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,0}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{8}
. For any fixed
j\in[m]
, we use Freedman’s inequality again to prove
\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,j}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{8m}
. We have

\displaystyle\left\|\textrm{Var}_{1}\left[\left.\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|=

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]\right\|\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right)\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\right\|\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]=\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right],

and

\displaystyle\textrm{Var}_{2}\left[\left.\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right\|^{2}\right|\mathcal{F}_{h,k}\right]=\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\textrm{Var}\left[\left.\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right|s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4G^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right),

where we have used
\textrm{Var}\left[\left.\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right|s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right]\leq\frac{4G^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
. Furthermore, notice that
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}^{j}X_{\theta,h}^{(k)}\right\|\leq\frac{2G\sqrt{C_{1}d}}{1-\gamma}
, the remaining steps will be exactly the same as those in the proof of the case
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,0}
. Taking a union bound over
j\in[m]
and
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,0}
, we have proved
\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{j=0}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon,j}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{4}
, which has finished the proof.
∎

Combining the results of Lemma
D.11
, Lemma
D.12
, Lemma
D.13
and take a union bound, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)\geq 1-\frac{3}{4}\delta.

Next, we prove some immediate results when the event
\mathcal{E}
holds.

Lemma D.14
.

When
\mathcal{E}_{\Sigma}
holds and

\displaystyle K\geq C_{1}d\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta},\quad\lambda\leq C_{1}d\sigma_{\textrm{min}}(\Sigma)\cdot\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta},

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta\Sigma^{-1}\right)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 4\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta}}{K}}.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta\Sigma^{-1}\right)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|=\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}-\Sigma^{-1}\right)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\Sigma}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}-I_{d}\right\|.

(28)

When
\mathcal{E}_{\Sigma}
holds, with the condition

\displaystyle K\geq C_{1}d\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta},\quad\lambda\leq C_{1}d\sigma_{\textrm{min}}(\Sigma)\cdot\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta},

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\Sigma}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}-I_{d}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{8dH}{\delta}}{K}}+\frac{2C_{1}d\log\frac{8dH}{\delta}}{3K}+\frac{\lambda\|\Sigma^{-1}\|}{K}\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta}}{K}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

which further implies
\sigma_{\textrm{min}}\left(\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\Sigma}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}
, and
\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 2
. Combining this result with (
28
), we get

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta\Sigma^{-1}\right)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 4\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta}}{K}}.

which has finished the proof.
∎

Lemma D.15
.

When
\mathcal{E}_{\Sigma}
and
\mathcal{E}_{Y}
hold, and

\displaystyle K\geq 36\kappa_{1}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})^{2}\frac{C_{1}d}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta},\quad\lambda\leq C_{1}d\sigma_{\textrm{min}}(\Sigma)\cdot\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta},

we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 6\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}}{K}},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)}{G}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 6\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}}{K}},\quad\forall j\in[m].

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|=\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\frac{1}{HK}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\int_{\mathcal{A}}\phi\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)},a^{\prime}\right)\pi_{\theta}\left(a^{\prime}\left|s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right.\right)\mathrm{d}a^{\prime}-M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}\left(\left(1+\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta\Sigma^{-1}\right)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\right)\left(1+\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\right)-1\right),

where
\Delta Y_{\theta}=\frac{1}{HK}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\phi_{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right)^{\top}-\Sigma M_{\theta}
and the last inequality uses Lemma
D.9
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)}{G}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}\left(\left(1+\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta\Sigma\right)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\right)\left(1+\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)}{G}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\right)-1\right).

Using the result of Lemma
D.14
the event
\mathcal{E}_{Y}
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta\Sigma^{-1}\right)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 4\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta}}{K}}\leq 1,

and
\forall j\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 2(\kappa_{2}+1)\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}}{K}}\leq 1,\quad\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)}{G}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 2(\kappa_{3}+1)\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}}{K}}\leq 1,

which implies

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 2\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}\left(\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta\Sigma^{-1}\right)\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|+\left\|\Sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta Y_{\theta}\right)\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\right)\leq 6\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}}{K}},

where we use the fact
(1+x_{1})(1+x_{2})-1\leq 2(x_{1}+x_{2})
whenever
x_{1},x_{2}\in[0,1]
. Similarly, we get

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)}{G}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 6\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}}{K}},\quad\forall j\in[m],

which has finished the proof.
∎

Lemma D.16
.

When
\mathcal{E}_{\Sigma}
and
\mathcal{E}_{Y}
hold, and

\displaystyle K\geq 36\kappa_{1}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})^{2}\frac{C_{1}d}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta},\quad\lambda\leq C_{1}d\sigma_{\textrm{min}}(\Sigma)\cdot\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta},

we have

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}^{\theta}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\widehat{r},\quad\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\tilde{U}^{\theta}.

Proof.

Firstly, note that given the conditions, the result of Lemma
D.15
implies

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 6\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\sqrt{\frac{C_{1}d\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}}{K}}\leq 1-\gamma.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\gamma\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{M}_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\leq\gamma\left(\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{M}_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|+\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Delta\widehat{M}_{\theta}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\right)\leq\gamma(2-\gamma)<1.

where we use the result of Lemma
D.10
to get
\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{M}_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq 1
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left(I_{d}-\gamma\widehat{M}_{\theta}\right)^{-1}=\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(I_{d}-\gamma\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{M}_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}=\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{M}_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{h-1}\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\widehat{M}_{\theta}^{h-1}.

Based on this result, we prove the main result by definition:

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}^{\theta}(\cdot,\cdot)

\displaystyle=\phi(\cdot,\cdot)^{\top}\left(I_{d}-\gamma\widehat{M}_{\theta}\right)^{-1}\widehat{w}_{r}=\phi(\cdot,\cdot)^{\top}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\widehat{M}_{\theta}^{h-1}\widehat{w}_{r}=\left(\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\widehat{r}\right)(\cdot,\cdot),

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}}(\cdot,\cdot)

\displaystyle=\phi(\cdot,\cdot)^{\top}\left(I_{d}-\gamma\widehat{M}_{\theta}\right)^{-1}\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}}\widehat{w}^{\theta}=\phi(\cdot,\cdot)^{\top}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\widehat{M}_{\theta}^{h-1}\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}}\widehat{w}^{\theta}=\left(\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\tilde{U}^{\theta}\right)(\cdot,\cdot),

which has finished the proof.
∎

Now we consider the decomposition of
Q^{\theta}-\widehat{Q}^{\theta}
:

Lemma D.17
.

Under the same condition of Lemma
D.16
, we have

\displaystyle Q^{\theta}-\widehat{Q}^{\theta}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{r}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right).

Proof.

Simply note that

\displaystyle Q^{\theta}-\widehat{Q}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}r-\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\widehat{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\right)r+\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(r-\widehat{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h^{\prime}-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}-\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-h^{\prime}-1}r+\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(r-\widehat{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h^{\prime}-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}-\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)\sum_{h=h^{\prime}+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-h^{\prime}-1}r+\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(r-\widehat{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}-\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)Q^{\theta}+\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(r-\widehat{r}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{r}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right),

which is the desired result.
∎

D.6
Proofs of Main Theorems

Define
\widehat{\nu}^{\theta}_{h}:=\left(\widehat{M}_{\theta}^{\top}\right)^{h-1}\nu_{1}^{\theta}
and
\widehat{\nu}^{\theta}=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\widehat{\nu}_{h}^{\theta}
. We may prove the following decomposition of
\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}-\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}}
:

Lemma D.18
.

Given the same condition of Lemma
D.16
, we have
\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}-\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}}=E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3}
, where

\displaystyle E_{1}=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}\left[\left(\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\frac{1}{KH}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right]

\displaystyle E_{2}=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}\left[\left(\left(\widehat{\nu}^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}-\left(\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\right)\frac{1}{KH}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right]

\displaystyle E_{3}=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{KH}\sum_{h=1}^{T}\nabla_{\theta}\left[\left(\widehat{\nu}^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}w^{\theta}\right].

The proof of Lemma
D.18
is deferred to appendix
D.7
. Based on this observation, here we show the proofs of our main theorems.

D.6.1
Proof of Theorem
D.3

Proof.

We use Lemma
D.18
to decompose
\langle\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}-\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}},t\rangle=\langle E_{1},t\rangle+\langle E_{2},t\rangle+\langle E_{3},t\rangle
. To bound each term individually, we introduce the following lemmas, whose proofs are deferred to appendix
D.7
.

Lemma D.19
.

For any
t\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{4}
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle E_{1},t\rangle|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t\log(8/\delta)}{HK}}+\frac{2\log(8/\delta)\sqrt{C_{1}md}\|t\|B}{3HK}.

where
B=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\max_{j\in[m]}\sqrt{\left(\nabla^{j}_{\theta}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla^{j}_{\theta}\nu^{\theta}}+\frac{2G}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sqrt{\left(\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}}
.

Lemma D.20
.

Let
E_{2}^{j}
be the
j
th entry of
E_{2}
, suppose
\mathcal{E}
holds and
K\geq 36\kappa_{1}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})^{2}C_{1}d(1-\gamma)^{-2}\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}
and
\lambda\leq C_{1}d\sigma_{\min}(\Sigma)\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta}
, then we have

\displaystyle|E_{2}^{j}|\leq\frac{240\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}(2+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\sqrt{C_{1}}d}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}\left(\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|+\frac{G}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\right)\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\frac{\log\frac{32dmH}{\delta}}{KH},\quad\forall j\in[m].

Lemma D.21
.

Let
E_{3}^{j}
be the
j
th entry of
E_{3}
, suppose
\mathcal{E}
holds and
K\geq 36\kappa_{1}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})^{2}C_{1}d(1-\gamma)^{-2}\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}
and
\lambda\leq C_{1}d\sigma_{\min}(\Sigma)\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle|E_{3}^{j}|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{6C_{1}d}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left(\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|+\frac{G}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\right)\frac{\log\frac{8dmH}{\delta}}{KH},\quad\forall j\in[m].

Let
B_{1}^{j}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sqrt{\left(\nabla^{j}_{\theta}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla^{j}_{\theta}\nu^{\theta}},\ B_{2}=\frac{G}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sqrt{\left(\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}}
, then we have the relation
B=\max_{j\in[m]}B_{1}^{j}+2B_{2}
. For any
j\in[m]
, note that

\displaystyle B_{1}^{j}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}\right\|\leq\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\frac{\gamma^{h-1}}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\left(\left(M_{\theta}^{h-1}\right)^{\top}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\frac{\gamma^{h-1}}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\left(\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|^{h-1}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|+(h-1)\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|^{h-2}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}M_{\theta}\right)\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left(\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|+\frac{G}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\right),

where we use the result of Lemma
D.10
. Similarly,

\displaystyle B_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\frac{G\gamma^{h-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{h}\right\|\leq\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\frac{G\gamma^{h-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}M_{\theta}\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|^{h-1}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\frac{G\gamma^{h-1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\leq\frac{G}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|.

We conclude that when
K\geq 36C_{1}d(1-\gamma)^{-2}\kappa_{1}(4+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})^{2}\log\frac{16dmH}{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\max_{j\in[m]}B_{1}^{j}+2B_{2}\right)\frac{2\log\frac{2}{\delta}\sqrt{C_{1}md}\|t\|}{KH}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\left(\max_{j\in[m]}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|+2HG\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\right)\frac{2\log\frac{2}{\delta}\sqrt{C_{1}md}\|t\|}{KH},

Now, take a union bound on
\mathcal{E}
and the event in Lemma
D.19
, we get with probability
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle E_{1},t\rangle|+|\langle E_{2},t\rangle|+|\langle E_{3},t\rangle|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t\log(8/\delta)}{HK}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}(5+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\left(\max_{j\in[m]}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|+\frac{G}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\right)\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\frac{240C_{1}d\log\frac{32dmH}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}KH}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t\log(8/\delta)}{KH}}+\kappa_{1}(5+\kappa_{2}+\kappa_{3})\left(\max_{j\in[m]}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|+\frac{G}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma_{\theta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}_{1}\right\|\right)\frac{240C_{1}d\sqrt{m}\|t\|\log\frac{32dmH}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)^{3}HK}.

we have finished the proof.
∎

D.6.2
Proof of Theorem
D.4

Proof.

According to the result of Theorem
D.3
, we know

\displaystyle|\langle t,\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\rangle|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t}{HK}\cdot\log\frac{8}{\delta}}+\frac{C_{\theta}\|t\|\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}}{HK},

Pick
t=e_{j},j\in[m]
, we have

\displaystyle t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\left(\varepsilon^{\theta}_{h,1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\varepsilon^{\theta}_{h,1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(\varepsilon^{\theta}_{h,1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{G^{2}}{H(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left(\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{1}{H(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(\frac{G^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}\right\|^{2}\right).

Therefore, take a union bound over
j\in[m]
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}^{j}v_{\theta}\right|\leq 4b_{\theta}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{8m}{\delta}}{HK}}+\frac{2C_{\theta}\sqrt{m}\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}}{HK},\quad\forall j\in[m],

where

\displaystyle b_{\theta}=\frac{G}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}\right\|.

When
\phi(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s,a)\geq 0,\ \forall(s,a),(s^{\prime},a^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, for any
h\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\nabla^{j}_{\theta}\nu^{\theta}_{h}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s,a)\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s,a)\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\log\pi_{\theta,h^{\prime}}(a_{h^{\prime}}|s_{h^{\prime}})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s,a)\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h}\left|\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\log\pi_{\theta,h^{\prime}}(a_{h^{\prime}}|s_{h^{\prime}})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Gh\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s,a)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Gh\left(\nu^{\theta}_{h}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s,a).

Meanwhile, for any positive integer
\tilde{H}
, we have

\displaystyle\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu_{h}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\tilde{H}}\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu_{h}^{\theta}+\sum_{h=\tilde{H}+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu_{h}^{\theta}.

For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=\tilde{H}+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu_{h}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=\tilde{H}+1}^{\infty}Gh\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu_{h}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq GC_{1}d\sum_{h=\tilde{H}+1}^{\infty}h\gamma^{h-1}

\displaystyle=GC_{1}d\left(\tilde{H}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\right)\gamma^{\tilde{H}}.

For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\tilde{H}}\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu_{h}^{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq G\tilde{H}\sum_{h=1}^{\tilde{H}}\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu_{h}^{\theta}\leq G\tilde{H}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu_{h}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=G\tilde{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t=

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{G^{2}}{H(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left(\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{1}{H(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{G^{2}}{H(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\tilde{H}^{2}+\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\right)\left(\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{4G^{2}C_{1}^{2}d^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\tilde{H}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\right)^{2}\gamma^{2\tilde{H}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4G^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\tilde{H}+\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\right)^{2}\left(\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|^{2}+C_{1}^{2}d^{2}\gamma^{2\tilde{H}}\right).

In particular, pick

\displaystyle\tilde{H}=\frac{2\log(C_{1}d)}{1-\gamma}\geq\frac{\log\frac{C_{1}d}{\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|}}{\log\frac{1}{\gamma}},

where we use the fact
\log\frac{1}{\gamma}\geq\frac{1-\gamma}{2\gamma}
whenever
\gamma\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
, and

\displaystyle\left(\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}=\sup_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\left(w^{\top}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{2}}{w^{\top}\Sigma w}\geq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\geq 1,

where the inequality is due to the fact that
\mathcal{F}
includes the constant functions. We have

\displaystyle t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t\leq\frac{8G^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\tilde{H}+\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)}\right)^{2}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{128G^{2}\left(\log(C_{1}d)\right)^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|^{2}.

Taking a union bound w.r.t.
m
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}^{j}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}^{j}v_{\theta}\right|\leq\frac{16G\log(C_{1}d)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\right\|\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{8m}{\delta}}{HK}}+\frac{2C_{\theta}\sqrt{m}\log\frac{32mdH}{\delta}}{HK}.

∎

D.6.3
Proof of Theorem
D.5

Proof.

We use the same decomposition as in Theorem
D.3
. Define a martingale difference sequence
\{e_{k}^{\theta}\}_{k=1}^{K}
by

\displaystyle e_{h,k}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{HK}}\nabla_{\theta}\left(\left(\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)})\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{HK}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)})\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{HK}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(\nu^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)})\nabla_{\theta}\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta},

we have

\displaystyle\|e_{h,k}^{\theta}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{HK}(1-\gamma)}\max_{j\in[m]}\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla_{\theta}^{j}\nu^{\theta}\|\sqrt{C_{1}d}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{HK}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\|\Sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\theta}\|\sqrt{C_{1}d}G\rightarrow 0,

where we use the result of Lemma
D.8
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}\left[e_{h,k}^{\theta}\left(e_{h,k}^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\right]_{ij}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{HK}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left[\nabla_{\theta_{1}}^{i}\left(\left(\nu^{\theta_{1}}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)})\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta_{1}}\right)\right]\left[\nabla_{\theta_{2}}^{j}\left(\left(\nu^{\theta_{2}}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)})\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta_{2}}\right)\right]^{\top}\right]\Bigg|_{\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}=\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{[\Lambda_{\theta}]_{ij}}{K}.

Therefore,by WLLN, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left[e_{h,k}^{\theta}\left(e_{h,k}^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\right]_{ij}\rightarrow_{p}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}\left[e_{h,k}^{\theta}\left(e_{h,k}^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\right]_{ij}=\left[\Lambda_{\theta}\right]_{ij},

To finish the rest of the proof, we introduce the following lemmas,

Lemma D.22

(Martingale CLT, Corollary 2.8 in (McLeish et al., 1974))
.

Let
\left\{X_{mn},n=1,\ldots,k_{m}\right\}
be a martingale difference array (row-wise) on the probability triple
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P)
.Suppose
X_{mn}
satisfy the following two conditions:

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq n\leq k_{m}}\left|X_{mn}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\rightarrow}}0,\textrm{ and }\sum_{n=1}^{k_{m}}X_{mn}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\rightarrow}}\sigma^{2}

for
k_{m}\rightarrow\infty
. Then
\sum_{n=1}^{k_{m}}X_{mn}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\sigma^{2}\right)
.

Lemma D.23

(Cramér–Wold Theorem)
.

Let
X_{n}=(X_{n}^{1},X_{n}^{2},\ldots,X_{n}^{k})^{\top}
be a
k
-dimensional random vector series and
X=(X^{1},X^{2},\ldots,X^{k})^{\top}
be a random vector of same dimension. Then
X_{n}
converges in distribution to
X
if and only if for any constant vector
t=(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{k})^{\top}
,
t^{\top}X_{n}
converges to
t^{\top}X
in distribution.

Lemma
D.22
implies
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}t^{\top}e_{h,k}\rightarrow_{d}\mathcal{N}(0,t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t)
for any
t
, and Lemma
D.23
implies

\displaystyle\sqrt{HK}E_{1}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}e_{h,k}\rightarrow_{p}\mathcal{N}(0,\Lambda_{\theta}).

Furthermore, notice that the results of Lemma
D.20
and Lemma
D.21
imply
\sqrt{HK}E_{2}\rightarrow_{p}0,\sqrt{HK}E_{3}\rightarrow_{p}0
. Combining the above results, we have finished the proof.
∎

D.6.4
Proof of Theorem
D.6

Proof.

We first derive the influence function of policy gradient estimator for sake of completeness. We denote each of the
K
sampled trajectories as

\bm{\tau}:=\left(s_{1},a_{1},r_{1},s_{2},a_{2},r_{2},\ldots,s_{H},a_{H},r_{H},s_{H+1}\right)

We denote
\bar{\pi}(a\mid s)
as the behavior policy. The distribution of trajectory is then given by

\mathcal{P}(d\bm{\tau})=\bar{\xi}\left(ds_{1},da_{1}\right)p\left(ds_{2}\mid s_{1},a_{1}\right)\bar{\pi}\left(da_{2}\mid s_{2}\right)\ldots\bar{\pi}\left(da_{H}\mid s_{H}\right)p\left(ds_{H+1}\mid s_{H},a_{H}\right)

Define
p_{\eta}=p+\eta\Delta p
as a new transition probability function and
\mathcal{P}_{\eta}:=\mathcal{P}+\eta\Delta\mathcal{P}
where
\Delta\mathcal{P}
satisfies

(\Delta\mathcal{P})\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{F}.

Define
g_{\eta}\left(s^{\prime}\mid s,a\right):=\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\log p_{\eta}\left(s^{\prime}\mid s,a\right)
and the score function as

g_{\eta}(\bm{\tau}):=\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\log\mathcal{P}_{\eta}(d\bm{\tau})=\sum_{h=1}^{H}g_{\eta}\left(s_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h}\right).

Without loss of generality, we assume
p_{\eta}
is continuously derivative with respect to
\eta.
This guarantees that we can change the order of taking derivatives with respect to
\eta
and
\theta.
When the subscript
\eta
vanishes, it means
\eta=0
and the underlying transition probability is
p(s^{\prime}|s,a),
i.e.
p_{0}(s^{\prime}|s,a)=p(s^{\prime}|s,a).
Then we denote
g(s^{\prime}|s,a):=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\log p_{\eta}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\right|_{\eta=0},
and
g(\bm{\tau})=\sum_{h=1}^{H}g(s_{h+1}|s_{h},a_{h}).
We define the policy value under new transition kernel is

v_{\theta,\eta}:=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}r(s_{h},a_{h})\right|s_{1}\sim\xi,\mathcal{P}_{\eta}\right]

Then, our objective function is

\psi_{\eta}:=\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta,\eta}=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\nabla_{\theta}\log\pi_{\theta}\left(a_{h}\mid s_{h}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{h^{\prime}=h}^{\infty}\gamma^{h^{\prime}-1}r\left(s_{h^{\prime}},a_{h^{\prime}}\right)\right)\right|s_{1}\sim\xi,\mathcal{P}_{\eta}\right].

We are going to compute the influence function with respect to the above objective function. We denote this influence function as
\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau}).
By definition, it satisfies that

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\psi_{\eta}\right|_{\eta=0}=\mathbb{E}\left[g(\bm{\tau})\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})\right].

By exchanging the order of derivatives, we find that

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\psi_{\eta}\right|_{\eta=0}=\nabla_{\theta}\left[\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}v_{\theta,\eta}\right|_{\eta=0}\right].

Therefore, we calculate the derivatives.

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}v_{\theta,\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\int_{(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{h}}r\left(s_{h},a_{h}\right)\xi(s_{1})\prod_{j=1}^{h-1}p_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{h}\pi_{\theta}\left(a_{j}\mid s_{j}\right)d\bm{\tau}_{h}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\int_{(\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A})^{h}}r\left(s_{h},a_{h}\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}g_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)\right)\xi(s_{1})\prod_{j=1}^{h-1}p_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{h}\pi_{\theta}\left(a_{j}\mid s_{j}\right)d\bm{\tau}_{h}

\displaystyle=\int\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}r\left(s_{h},a_{h}\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}g_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)\right)\left[\xi(s_{1})\prod_{j=1}^{h}p_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{h}\pi_{\theta}\left(a_{j}\mid s_{j}\right)\right]d\bm{\tau}.

We denote
Q_{\eta}^{\theta}
and
\nabla_{\theta}Q_{\eta}^{\theta}
as the state-action function and its gradient with underlying transition probability being
p_{\eta}.
For sake of simplicity, we define the state value function as

V^{\theta}(s):=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}r(s_{h},a_{h})\right|s_{1}=s,\mathcal{P}\right].

We denote
V_{\eta}^{\theta}(s)
as the same function except for transition probability substituted by
p_{\eta}.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}v_{\theta,\eta}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}r\left(s_{h},a_{h}\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}g_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)\right)\right|s_{1}\sim\xi,\mathcal{P}_{\eta}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}g_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)\sum_{h=j+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}r\left(s_{h},a_{h}\right)\right|s_{1}\sim\xi,\mathcal{P}_{\eta}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}g_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{h=j+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}r\left(s_{h},a_{h}\right)\right|s_{j+1}\right]\right|s_{1}\sim\xi,\mathcal{P}_{\eta}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\gamma^{j}g_{\eta}\left(s_{j+1}\mid s_{j},a_{j}\right)V_{\eta}^{\theta}(s_{j+1})\right|s_{j},a_{j}\right]\right|s_{1}\sim\xi,\mathcal{P}_{\eta}\right].

Therefore,

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}v_{\theta,\eta}\right|_{\eta=0}=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\left.\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\gamma^{h}g\left(s_{h+1}\mid s_{h},a_{h}\right)V^{\theta}(s_{h+1})\right|s_{h},a_{h}\right]\right|s_{1}\sim\xi,\mathcal{P}_{\eta}\right].

(29)

We notice that
\Sigma=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\right]
. We denote
w_{h}(s,a):=\phi^{\top}(s,a)\Sigma^{-1}\nu_{h}^{\theta}=\phi^{\top}(s,a)\Sigma^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\mid s_{1}\sim\xi\right].
We leverage the following fact to rewrite (
29
): for any
f(s,a)=w_{f}^{\top}\phi(s,a)\in\mathcal{F}
where
w_{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[f(s_{h},a_{h})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[w_{f}^{\top}\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[w_{f}^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\phi^{\top}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\right]\Sigma^{-1}\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}w_{f}^{\top}\phi\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\phi^{\top}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\Sigma^{-1}\mathbb{E}^{\pi_{\theta}}\left[\phi(s_{h},a_{h})\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}f\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)w_{h}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\right]

Since

\mathbb{E}\left[g\left(s^{\prime}\mid s,a\right)V^{\theta}(s^{\prime})|s,a\right]=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\left(Q_{\eta}^{\theta}(s,a)-r_{\eta}(s,a)\right)\right|_{\eta=0}\in\mathcal{F},

we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}v_{\theta,\eta}\right|_{\eta=0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}w_{h}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[g\left(s^{\prime}\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\cdot V^{\theta}\left(s^{\prime}\right)\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim p(\cdot\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)})}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}w_{h}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)g\left(s^{\prime}\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\cdot V^{\theta}\left(s^{\prime}\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim p(\cdot\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)})}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}w_{h}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)g\left(s^{\prime}\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\left(V^{\theta}\left(s^{\prime}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\theta}\left(s^{\prime}\right)\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right]\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}w_{h}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)g\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\left(V^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\right)\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right]\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[g\left(\bm{\tau}\right)\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}w_{h}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\left(V^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\right)\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right]\right)\right].

Taking gradient in both sides and we have

\nabla_{\theta}\left(\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}v_{\theta,\eta}\right|_{\eta=0}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left\{g\left(\bm{\tau}\right)\cdot\nabla_{\theta}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\gamma^{h}w_{h}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\left(V^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\right)\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right]\right)\right]\right\}.

The implies that the influence function we want is

\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})=\nabla_{\theta}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}w_{h}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)\left(V^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[V^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(1)}\right)\mid s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right]\right)\right].

Insert the expression of
w_{h}(s,a)
and exploit
\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}=Q^{\theta}(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)})-r_{h}^{(k)}-\gamma\int_{\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\theta}\left(a^{\prime}\mid s_{h+1}^{(k)}\right)Q^{\theta}\left(s_{h+1}^{(k)},a^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{d}a^{\prime},
we can rewrite the influence function as

\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})=-\nabla_{\theta}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\phi\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\varepsilon_{h^{\prime},1}^{\theta}\nu_{h}^{\theta}\right]=-\nabla_{\theta}\left[\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\varepsilon_{h,1}^{\theta}\nu^{\theta}\right]

Therefore, since the cross terms vanish by taking conditional expectation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})^{\top}\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})\right]=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{H^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\left(\varepsilon^{\theta}_{h,1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)\right)^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}\left(\varepsilon^{\theta}_{h,1}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(1)},a_{h}^{(1)}\right)^{\top}\Sigma^{-1}\nu^{\theta}\right)\Bigg]=\frac{1}{H}\Lambda_{\theta}.

For any vector
t\in\mathbb{R}^{m},
when it comes to
\left\langle t,\psi_{\eta}\right\rangle,
by linearity we have

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta}\left\langle t,\psi_{\eta}\right\rangle\right|_{\eta=0}=\mathbb{E}[g(\bm{\tau})\left\langle t,\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})\right\rangle].

Then the influence function of
\left\langle t,\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\right\rangle
is
\left\langle t,\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})\right\rangle.
The Cramer-Rao lower bound for
\left\langle t,\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\right\rangle
is

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle t,\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})\right\rangle^{2}\right]=t^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})^{\top}\mathcal{I}(\bm{\tau})\right]t=\frac{1}{H}t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t.

By continuous mapping theorem, a trivial corollary of Theorem
D.6
is that for any
t\in\mathbb{R}^{m},

\sqrt{HK}\left(\left\langle t,\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}}-\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\right\rangle\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t\right).

This implies that the variance of any unbiased estimator for
\left\langle t,\nabla_{\theta}v_{\theta}\right\rangle\in\mathbb{R}
is lower bounded by
\frac{1}{\sqrt{HK}}t^{\top}\Lambda_{\theta}t.

∎

D.7
Missing Proofs

D.7.1
Proof of Lemma
D.18

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}-\widehat{\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}U^{\theta}-\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\tilde{U}^{\theta}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}U^{\theta}-\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\widehat{U}^{\theta}+\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(\widehat{U}^{\theta}-\tilde{U}^{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}-\widehat{U}^{\theta}-\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right)+\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(\widehat{U}^{\theta}-\tilde{U}^{\theta}\right).

For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}-\widehat{U}^{\theta}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\phi^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\frac{1}{KH}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(k)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(k)}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(k)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(k)}\right)-\gamma\int_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\left(\nabla_{\theta}\pi_{\theta}\left(a^{\prime}\left|s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(k)}\right.\right)\right)Q^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(k)},a^{\prime}\right)+\pi_{\theta}\left(a^{\prime}\left|s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(k)}\right.\right)\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\left(s_{h^{\prime}+1}^{(k)},a^{\prime}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}a^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{KH}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\phi^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}w^{\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\phi^{\top}\left(\widehat{M}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\frac{1}{KH}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h^{\prime}}^{(k)},a_{h^{\prime}}^{(k)}\right)\nabla_{\theta}\varepsilon_{h^{\prime},k}^{\theta}+\frac{\lambda}{KH}\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\phi^{\top}\left(\widehat{M}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}w^{\theta}.

Using the definition of
\widehat{\nu}^{\theta}
, we get

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}\xi(s)\pi_{\theta}(a|s)\left(\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}-\widehat{U}^{\theta}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\nabla_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right)\right)(s,a)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}a\\
=&\left(\widehat{\nu}^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\frac{1}{KH}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\phi\left(s_{h}^{(k)},a_{h}^{(k)}\right)\nabla_{\theta}\varepsilon_{h,k}^{\theta}+\frac{\lambda}{KH}\left(\widehat{\nu}^{\theta}\right)^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}w^{\theta}.\end{aligned}

(30)

For the second term, by Lemma
D.17
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(\widehat{U}^{\theta}-\tilde{U}^{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)\left(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{Q}^{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\gamma^{h}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)\sum_{h^{\prime}=h}^{\infty}\gamma^{h^{\prime}-h-1}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h^{\prime}-h-1}\left(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{r}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h^{\prime}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-h^{\prime}-1}\left(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{r}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right).

Meanwhile, again by Lemma
D.17
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{Q}^{\theta})=\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{r}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right),

which implies

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}\left(\widehat{U}^{\theta}-\tilde{U}^{\theta}\right)+\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{Q}^{\theta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\left(\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-1}+\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h^{\prime}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-h^{\prime}-1}\right)\left(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{r}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{\infty}\sum_{h^{\prime}=0}^{h-1}\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h^{\prime}}\left(\nabla_{\theta}\log\Pi_{\theta}\right)\left(\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}\right)^{h-h^{\prime}-1}\left(Q^{\theta}-\widehat{r}-\gamma\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\theta}Q^{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=
